An elevated Cleveland Clinic (CC) score is indicative of a high pre-test probability of identifying a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.yehia.2020.r150">Yehia &#x00026; Eng 2020</a>]. </p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="phts.Management"><h2 id="_phts_Management_">Management</h2><p>Clinical practice guidelines for <i>PTEN</i> hamartoma tumor syndrome (PHTS) have been published and are renewed annually by the US-based National Comprehensive Cancer Network (NCCN Guidelines, <a href="https://www.nccn.org/guidelines/guidelines-detail?category=2&#x00026;id=1503" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Genetic/Familial High-Risk Assessment: Breast, Ovarian, and Pancreatic Version 3.2026</a> (login required; accessed 3-29-26). More recently, international consensus guidelines for the surveillance and management of cancer and overgrowth as well as neurodevelopmental and neurologic manifestations in PHTS have been published, aiming at unifying guidelines and improving care for affected individuals and families [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.dhawan.2025a.1754">Dhawan et al 2025a</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.dhawan.2025b.e200299">Dhawan et al 2025b</a>].</p><div id="phts.Evaluations_and_Surveillance_Guidel"><h3>Evaluations and Surveillance Guidelines Following Initial Diagnosis</h3><p>To establish the extent of disease and needs of an individual diagnosed with PHTS, the evaluations summarized in <a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobphtsTptenhamartomatumorsyndromerec">Table 4</a> (if not performed as part of the evaluation that led to the diagnosis) are recommended. </p><div id="phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 4. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>PTEN</i> Hamartoma Tumor Syndrome: Recommended Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation </th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comment <sup>1</sup></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>General</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Complete medical history &#x00026; family history for features of PHTS</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Comprehensive physical exam</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Education re signs &#x00026; symptoms of cancer</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 18 yrs, or 5 yrs before youngest age of diagnosis of a PHTS-related cancer in family (whichever comes 1st)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dermatologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Dermatologic exam (incl for cutaneous melanoma)</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At diagnosis</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Thyroid</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thyroid exam</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thyroid US</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 12 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Breast (females)</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Education re breast awareness &#x00026; self-exam</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 18 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Clinical breast exam</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 25 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Mammogram w/consideration of tomosynthesis &#x00026; breast MRI w/contrast</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 30 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Kidney</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Kidney US</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider kidney CT or MRI.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 40 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endometrium</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Education re prompt response to symptoms (e.g., abnormal bleeding)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider endometrial biopsy.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 35 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Transvaginal US</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In postmenopausal women at clinician's discretion &#x00026; as needed</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Colon</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for clinical manifestations&#x000a0;/ complications of GI hamartomatous polyps</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At diagnosis</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Colonoscopy</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Starting at age 35 yrs; earlier in those presenting w/concerning symptoms</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development/</b>
<br />
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Developmental assessment</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl motor, adaptive, cognitive, &#x00026; speech-language eval</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval for early intervention&#x000a0;/ special education</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess for clinical manifestations of seizures.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Refer to neurologist &#x00026; perform EEG if seizures are suspected.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neurologic exam for features of intracranial AVMs (e.g., motor/sensory findings)</div></li></ul>
Note: Routine brain imaging is not recommended in persons who have no concerning signs/symptoms.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Brain MRA/MRV &#x00026; MRI w/IV contrast (gadolinium) in those w/concerning neurologic findings (positional or projectile emesis, abnormal exam, new neurologic findings, manifestations of increased intracranial pressure, or new-onset headaches in child age &#x0003c;5 yrs) to assess for structural etiologies (e.g., intracranial AVM, tumors, focal cortical dysplasia)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In those w/suspected AVM, use hemosiderin-specific sequences (e.g., GRE &#x00026; SWI) for cavernous malformations; consider conventional angiography; refer to multidisciplinary vascular team.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Educate individuals/caregivers re potential developmental/neurologic disorders.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Incl ID, learning differences, &#x00026; less common features (e.g., seizures, Lhermitte-Duclos disease, vasculopathy)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurobehavioral/</b>
<br />
<b>Psychiatric</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Neuropsychiatric eval</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For persons age &#x0003e;12 mos: screening for concerns incl findings suggestive of ASD</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Educate individuals/caregivers re neurobehavioral/psychiatric complications.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Incl ASD, mood/behavioral &#x00026; sleep issues, &#x00026; ADHD</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Musculoskeletal features</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for hypotonia &#x00026; scoliosis.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In those w/BRRS <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Genetic counseling</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By genetics professionals&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To obtain a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pedigree/">pedigree</a> &#x00026; inform affected persons &#x00026; their families re nature, MOI, &#x00026; implications of PHTS to facilitate medical &#x00026; personal decision making</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family support</b>
<br />
<b>&#x00026; resources</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By clinicians, wider care team, &#x00026; family support organizations</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Psychosocial support as needed (e.g., to address diagnosis, family planning, risk-reducing mastectomy)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ADHD = attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder; ASD = autism spectrum disorder; AVM = arteriovenous malformations; BRRS = Bannayan-Riley-Ruvalcaba syndrome; GRE = gradient-recalled echo; ID = intellectual disability; IV = intravenous; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; MRA = magnetic resonance angiography; MRI = magnetic resonance imaging; MRV = magnetic resonance venography; PHTS = <i>PTEN</i> hamartoma tumor syndrome; SWI = susceptibility-weighted imaging; US = ultrasound</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.4.1"><p class="no_margin">For individuals with a family history of a particular cancer type at an early age, surveillance should be considered five to ten years prior to the youngest diagnosis in the family.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.4.2"><p class="no_margin">Clinical geneticist, certified genetic counselor, certified genetic nurse, genetics advanced practice provider (nurse practitioner or physician assistant)</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="phts.Treatment_of_Manifestations"><h3>Treatment of Manifestations</h3><p>Supportive care to improve quality of life, maximize function, and reduce complications is recommended. This ideally involves multidisciplinary care by specialists in relevant fields (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobphtsTptenhamartomatumorsyndrometre">Table 5</a>).</p><div id="phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 5. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>PTEN</i> Hamartoma Tumor Syndrome: Treatment of Manifestations</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Skin &#x00026; mucosal lesions</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Asymptomatic lesions: observation alone is prudent.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Cutaneous lesions should be excised only if malignancy is suspected or symptoms (e.g., pain, deformity, scarring) are significant.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Symptomatic lesions: topical agents (e.g., 5-fluorouracil), curettage, cryosurgery, or laser ablation may provide only temporary relief.&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Surgical excision is sometimes complicated by keloid formation &#x00026; recurrence (often rapid) of the lesions.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastrointestinal polyps/cancer</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Endoscopy w/removal of polyps per gastroenterologist</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment for colorectal carcinoma</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Thyroid disease</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thyroid US to monitor for increase in size of nodules or multinodular goiter</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If results of fine-needle aspiration are suspicious for malignancy, perform total thyroidectomy.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Subtotal thyroidectomies will lead to regrowth of thyroid w/similar cancer risks.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Fibrocystic breast disease&#x000a0;/ Breast cancer</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment per breast cancer specialist</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Discuss prophylactic mastectomy as needed.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Women may consider prophylactic mastectomy, esp if breast tissue is dense or repeated biopsies have been necessary. Prophylactic mastectomy reduces breast cancer risk by 90% in women at high risk.&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup> The recommendation of prophylactic mastectomy is a generalization for women at increased risk for breast cancer from a variety of causes.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>No direct evidence supports use of tamoxifen or raloxifene in persons w/PHTS to reduce risk of breast cancer. Physicians should discuss limitations of evidence, risks, &#x00026; benefits of chemoprophylaxis individually. Clinician must discuss increased risk for endometrial cancer assoc w/tamoxifen in a population at increased risk for endometrial cancer.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Renal cell carcinoma</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endometrial fibroids/cancer</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Discuss preventative hysterectomy on completion of childbearing.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Multiple fibroids are treated w/total abdominal hysterectomy.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Single fibroid removals result in dramatic recurrences.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment for endometrial cancer</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Developmental delay&#x000a0;/ Intellectual disability&#x000a0;/ Neurobehavioral issues</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See <a href="#phts.Developmental_Delay__Intellectual_D">Developmental Delay&#x000a0;/ Intellectual Disability Management Issues</a>.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Seizures</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Mgmt w/anti-seizure medications per neurologist based on predominant seizure type&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Imaging to assess for epilepsy-assoc pathologies (e.g., focal cortical dysplasia &#x00026; tumors)</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider neuropsychiatric comorbidities&#x000a0;/ side effects.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Vascular anomalies&#x000a0;/ Soft tissue overgrowth</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Mgmt per multidisciplinary vascular team</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Lhermitte-Duclos disease (LDD)</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>For those w/atypical LDD,&#x000a0;<sup>5</sup> refer to neuro-oncologist, neurologist, &#x00026; neurosurgeon for biopsy/resection consideration.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Monitor those w/typical LDD (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobphtsTptenhamartomatumorsyndromerec1">Table 6</a>).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>For those w/suspected CSF obstruction, refer for surgical eval.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Musculoskeletal features</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment per orthopedist &#x00026; PT</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ensure appropriate social work involvement to connect families w/local resources, respite, &#x00026; support.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Coordinate care to manage multiple subspecialty appointments, equipment, medications, &#x00026; supplies.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_tre_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider involvement in adaptive sports or <a href="https://www.specialolympics.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Special Olympics</a>.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">CSF = cerebrospinal fluid; PHTS = <i>PTEN</i> hamartoma tumor syndrome; PT = physical therapist; US = ultrasound</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.5.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.hildenbrand.2001.362">Hildenbrand et al [2001]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.5.2"><p class="no_margin">C Eng, unpublished data</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.5.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.hartmann.1999.77">Hartmann et al [1999]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.5.4"><p class="no_margin">There is no evidence supporting the first-line use of mTOR inhibitors for epilepsy in individuals with PHTS.</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.5.5"><p class="no_margin">Atypical LDD: noncerebellar location, contrast enhancement, diffusion restriction, rapid growth. Typical LDD: cerebellar, T<sub>1</sub> hypointense, T<sub>2</sub> hyperintense, "tigroid"</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="phts.Developmental_Delay__Intellectual_D"><h4>Developmental Delay / Intellectual Disability Management Issues</h4><p>The following information represents typical management recommendations for individuals with developmental delay&#x000a0;/ intellectual disability in the United States; standard recommendations may vary from country to country. </p><p><b>Ages 0-3 years.</b> Referral to an early intervention program is recommended for access to occupational, physical, speech, and feeding therapy as well as infant mental health services, special educators, and sensory impairment specialists. In the US, early intervention is a federally funded program available in all states that provides in-home services to target individual therapy needs. </p><p><b>Ages 3-5 years.</b> In the US, developmental preschool through the local public school district is recommended. Before placement, an evaluation is made to determine needed services and therapies and an individualized education plan (IEP) is developed for those who qualify based on established motor, language, social, or cognitive delay. The early intervention program typically assists with this transition. Developmental preschool is center based; for children too medically unstable to attend, home-based services are provided. </p><p><b>All ages.</b> Consultation with a developmental pediatrician is recommended to ensure the involvement of appropriate community, state, and educational agencies (US) and to support parents in maximizing quality of life. Some issues to consider:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>IEP services:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>An IEP provides specially designed instruction and related services to children who qualify.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>IEP services will be reviewed annually to determine whether any changes are needed. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Special education law requires that children participating in an IEP be in the least restrictive environment feasible at school and included in general education as much as possible, when and where appropriate. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>PT, OT, and speech services will be provided in the IEP to the extent that the need affects the child's access to academic material. Beyond that, private supportive therapies based on the affected individual's needs may be considered. Specific recommendations regarding type of therapy can be made by a developmental pediatrician. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>As a child enters the teen years, a transition plan should be discussed and incorporated in the IEP. For those receiving IEP services, the public school district is required to provide services until age 21.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A 504 plan (Section 504: a US federal statute that prohibits discrimination based on disability) can be considered for those who require accommodations or modifications such as front-of-class seating, assistive technology devices, classroom scribes, extra time between classes, modified assignments, and enlarged text.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Developmental Disabilities Administration (DDA) enrollment is recommended. DDA is a US public agency that provides services and support to qualified individuals. Eligibility differs by state but is typically determined by diagnosis and/or associated cognitive/adaptive disabilities. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Families with limited income and resources may also qualify for supplemental security income (SSI) for their child with a disability. </div></li></ul></div><div id="phts.Motor_Dysfunction"><h4>Motor Dysfunction </h4><p>
<b>Gross motor dysfunction</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Physical therapy is recommended to maximize mobility and to reduce the risk for later-onset orthopedic complications (e.g., contractures, scoliosis, hip dislocation). </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider use of durable medical equipment and positioning devices as needed (e.g., wheelchairs, walkers, bath chairs, orthotics, adaptive strollers).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>For muscle tone abnormalities including hypertonia or dystonia, consider involving appropriate specialists to aid in management of baclofen, tizanidine, botulinum toxin, anti-parkinsonian medications, or orthopedic procedures.</div></li></ul><p><b>Fine motor dysfunction.</b> Occupational therapy is recommended for difficulty with fine motor skills that affect adaptive function such as feeding, grooming, dressing, and writing.</p><p><b>Speech, language, and communication issues.</b> Speech-language evaluation should be considered early in development for children who have delayed communication milestones or who are not yet talking. Evaluation for alternative means of communication (e.g., <a href="https://www.asha.org/NJC/AAC/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">augmentative and alternative communication</a> [AAC]) is appropriate for individuals who have speech or receptive and expressive language difficulties. An AAC evaluation should be completed by a speech-language pathologist who has expertise in the area. This evaluation typically takes into account cognitive abilities, sensory impairments, and motor skills to determine the most appropriate form of communication. AAC devices can range from low-tech, such as picture exchange communication, to high-tech, such as voice-generating devices. Contrary to popular belief, AAC devices do not hinder verbal development of speech, but rather support optimal speech and language development. Many children will continue to require AAC into later childhood and adulthood, while some may use their AAC for a shorter time to help aid speech and language development.</p></div><div id="phts.NeurobehavioralPsychiatric_Concerns"><h4>Neurobehavioral/Psychiatric Concerns</h4><p>Children may qualify for and benefit from interventions used in treatment of autism spectrum disorder, including applied behavior analysis (ABA). ABA therapy is targeted to the individual child's behavioral, social, and adaptive strengths and weaknesses and typically performed one on one with a board-certified behavior analyst. </p><p>Consultation with a developmental pediatrician may be helpful in guiding parents through appropriate behavior management strategies or providing prescription medications, such as medication used to treat attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder, when necessary. </p><p>Concerns about serious aggressive or destructive behavior can be addressed by a pediatric psychiatrist.</p></div></div><div id="phts.Surveillance"><h3>Surveillance</h3><p>The most serious consequences of PHTS relate to the increased risk of cancers. In this regard, the most important screening is increased cancer surveillance to detect any tumors at the earliest, most treatable stages. The evaluations summarized in <a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobphtsTptenhamartomatumorsyndromerec1">Table 6</a> are recommended.</p><div id="phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 6. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>PTEN</i> Hamartoma Tumor Syndrome: Recommended Surveillance</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK1488/table/phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Frequency&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>General</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comprehensive physical exam</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Education re signs &#x00026; symptoms of cancer</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually starting at age 18 yrs, or 5 yrs before youngest age of diagnosis of PHTS-related cancer in family (whichever comes 1st)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dermatologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Dermatologic exam (incl for cutaneous melanoma)</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Thyroid</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thyroid exam</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thyroid US</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually starting at age 12 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Breast (female)</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Breast self-exam; report changes to health care provider</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monthly starting at age 18 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Clinical breast exam</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 6-12 mos starting at age 25 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Mammogram w/consideration of tomosynthesis &#x00026; breast MRI w/contrast</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually starting at age 30 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Kidney</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Kidney US or other kidney imaging (CT or MRI preferred)</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider every 1-2 yrs starting at age 40 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endometrium</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess for symptoms (e.g., abnormal bleeding).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider endometrial biopsy.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 1-2 yrs starting by age 35 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Transvaginal US</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In postmenopausal women at clinician's discretion &#x00026; as needed</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastrointestinal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for clinical manifestations of polyps&#x000a0;/ colorectal carcinoma.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Colonoscopy</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 5 yrs starting at age 35 yrs; more frequently if person is symptomatic or polyps are found</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor developmental progress &#x00026; educational needs.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually throughout childhood &#x00026; adolescence</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurobehavioral/</b>
<br />
<b>Psychiatric</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment for ASD</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For persons age &#x0003e;12 mos: screening for concerns incl findings suggestive of ASD, as needed</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Lhermitte-Duclos disease (LDD)</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For those w/typical LDD&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Brain imaging every 3-12 mos</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Following LDD resection, monitor for recurrence w/targeted brain imaging using specialized MRI protocols</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 6-12 mos</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Meningioma</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For those w/identified meningioma:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Brain MRI &#x00026; exam following NCCN guidelines</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Involve multidisciplinary team for symptomatic/critical meningiomas</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At 3, 6, &#x00026; 12 mos after identification, then every 6-12 mos for 5 yrs, then every 1-3 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Scoliosis/</b>
<br />
<b>Asymmetry</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Clinical assessment for scoliosis &#x00026; asymmetry</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Spine radiographs as needed to monitor scoliosis</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually or as needed</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Other clinical features of Proteus syndrome</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See Proteus Syndrome, <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/proteus/#proteus.Surveillance">Surveillance</a>.</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess need for social work support (e.g., palliative/respite care, home nursing, other local resources), care coordination, or follow-up <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> if new questions arise (e.g., family planning). </td><td headers="hd_h_phts.T.pten_hamartoma_tumor_syndrome_rec_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="border-left: solid 0.50pt; border-top: solid 0.50pt; border-right: solid 0.50pt; border-bottom: solid 0.50pt;text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ASD = autism spectrum disorder; CSF = cerebrospinal fluid; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; NCCN = National Comprehensive Cancer Network; PHTS = <i>PTEN</i> hamartoma tumor syndrome; US = ultrasound</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.6.1"><p class="no_margin">For individuals with a family history of a particular cancer type at an early age, screening should be considered five to ten years prior to the youngest diagnosis in the family.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="phts.TF.6.2"><p class="no_margin">Typical LDD: asymptomatic, cerebellar, T<sub>1</sub> hypointense, T<sub>2</sub> hyperintense, "tigroid," no CSF obstruction</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="phts.AgentsCircumstances_to_Avoid"><h3>Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</h3><p>Because of the propensity for rapid tissue regrowth and the propensity to form keloid tissue, it is recommended that cutaneous lesions be excised only if malignancy is suspected or symptoms (e.g., pain, deformity) are significant.</p><p>Exercise caution with diagnostic/therapeutic radiation exposure; balance benefit versus potential secondary malignancy risk. MRI is not a risk factor.</p></div><div id="phts.Evaluation_of_Relatives_at_Risk"><h3>Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</h3><p>Molecular genetic testing for the <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is recommended for at-risk relatives of an affected individual. Family members who have the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>PTEN</i> pathogenic variant (and therefore have PHTS) require an <a href="#phts.Evaluations_and_Surveillance_Guidel">initial evaluation and ongoing surveillance</a>. </p><p>Although there is no consensus on the optimal timing of genetic testing of at-risk family members, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> for the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in early childhood is often appropriate because timely detection of early-onset manifestations such as thyroid disease and cancer can guide management. (The earliest age of documented <i>PTEN</i>-related breast cancer and thyroid cancer are age 17 years and age four years, respectively.) Neurodevelopmental features such as developmental delay and autism spectrum disorder may also benefit from early intervention. </p><p>Family members who have not inherited the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and their subsequent offspring have cancer risks similar to the general population.</p><p>See <a href="#phts.Related_Genetic_Counseling_Issues">Genetic Counseling</a> for issues related to testing of at-risk relatives for <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> purposes.</p></div><div id="phts.Therapies_Under_Investigation"><h3>Therapies Under Investigation</h3><p>Although mTOR inhibitors show promise for treatment of malignancies in individuals who have a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, use should be limited to clinical trials. A Phase II open-label clinical trial (<a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT00971789" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NCT00971789</a>) utilized the mTOR inhibitor sirolimus in adults with PHTS and showed evidence of improvement of clinical manifestations throughout the duration of the trial [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.komiya.2019.1510">Komiya et al 2019</a>]. A natural history study is completing accrual of pediatric, adolescent, and young adults with germline <i>PTEN</i> pathogenic variants and autism spectrum disorder (<a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT02461446" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NCT02461446</a>). Through this study, a double-blind drug-placebo crossover clinical trial investigated the mTOR inhibitor everolimus for its safety and potential neurocognitive benefits in individuals with PHTS (<a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT02991807" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NCT02991807</a>) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.srivastava.2022.3393">Srivastava et al 2022</a>]. The preliminary analysis showed that everolimus is well tolerated, but no significant improvement occurred on most neurocognitive and behavioral measures. However, several secondary efficacy endpoints moved in the direction of improvement, including measures of nonverbal IQ, verbal learning, autistic features, motor skills, adaptive behavior, and global improvement.</p><p>Search <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ClinicalTrials.gov</a> in the US and <a href="https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/search" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">EU Clinical Trials Register</a> in Europe for access to information on clinical studies for a wide range of diseases and conditions.</p></div></div><div id="phts.Genetic_Counseling"><h2 id="_phts_Genetic_Counseling_">Genetic Counseling</h2><p><i>Genetic counseling is the process of providing individuals and families with information on the nature, mode(s) of inheritance, and implications of genetic disorders to help them make informed medical and personal decisions. The following section deals with genetic risk assessment and the use of family history and genetic testing to clarify genetic status for family members; it is not meant to address all personal, cultural, or ethical issues that may arise or to substitute for consultation with a genetics professional</i>. &#x02014;ED.</p><div id="phts.Mode_of_Inheritance"><h3>Mode of Inheritance</h3><p><i>PTEN</i> hamartoma tumor syndrome (PHTS) &#x02013; which encompasses the historical clinical phenotypes of Cowden syndrome, Bannayan-Riley-Ruvalcaba syndrome, Proteus-like syndrome, and apparently <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/isolated/">isolated</a> adult-onset Lhermitte-Duclos disease &#x02013; is inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> manner. </p></div><div id="phts.Risk_to_Family_Members"><h3>Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Some individuals diagnosed with PHTS have an affected parent. Based on practical experience, about 50% of individuals diagnosed with PHTS have an affected relative [C Eng, unpublished data]. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Approximately 10%-44% of individuals diagnosed with PHTS have the disorder as the result of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.yehia.2020.103">Yehia et al 2020</a>]. Virtually all individuals with <i>PTEN</i>-related Proteus-like syndrome have a <i>de novo</i> pathogenic variant.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> appears to be the only affected family member (i.e., a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/simplex/">simplex</a> case), <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> is recommended for the parents of the proband to evaluate their genetic status, inform <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment, and determine their need for <a href="#phts.Evaluations_and_Surveillance_Guidel">evaluation and surveillance</a>. </div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Note: A <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> may appear to be the only affected family member because of failure to recognize the disorder in family members, early death of the parent before the onset of symptoms, or late onset of the disorder in a parent. Because external manifestations of PHTS may be subtle [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.yehia.2020.103">Yehia et al 2020</a>] or variably expressed (even among relatives), diagnoses within families are sometimes only recognized after identification of a more severely affected individual. Therefore, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> occurrence of a <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> cannot be confirmed unless <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> has demonstrated that neither parent is <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for the <i>PTEN</i> pathogenic variant identified in the proband.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is not identified in either parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, the following possibilities should be considered:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>. </div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> inherited a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> from a parent with gonadal (or somatic and gonadal) <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mosaicism/">mosaicism</a>.* Note: Testing of parental leukocyte DNA may not detect all instances of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/somatic-mosaicism/">somatic mosaicism</a> and will not detect a pathogenic variant that is present in the germ (gonadal) cells only.</div><div class="half_rhythm">*&#x000a0;A parent with somatic and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gonadal-mosaicism/">gonadal mosaicism</a> for a PTEN <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> may be mildly/minimally affected.</div></li></ul></li></ul><p><b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</b> The risk to the sibs of the proband depends on the genetic status of the proband's parents:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a parent of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has the <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the proband, the risk to the sibs of inheriting the pathogenic variant is 50%. The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/penetrance/">penetrance</a> of PHTS is close to 99% by adulthood in individuals with a <i>PTEN</i> pathogenic variant.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> cannot be detected in the leukocyte DNA of either parent, the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> to sibs is estimated to be 1% because of the possibility of parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gonadal-mosaicism/">gonadal mosaicism</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#phts.REF.zemet.2022.811">Zemet et al 2022</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the parents have not been tested for the <i>PTEN</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> but are clinically unaffected and are in their 30s, the risk to the sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> appears to be low. However, sibs of a proband with clinically unaffected parents are still presumed to be at increased risk for PHTS because of the possibility of age-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/penetrance/">penetrance</a> in a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> parent or parental <a
